Hangar #17 was used by [Port Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_of_New_York_and_New_Jersey) to store 2,500 artifacts from the [World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(1973%E2%80%932001\)) site after the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md).

Three chapels, including [Our Lady of the Skies Chapel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Lady_of_the_Skies_Chapel_at_John_F._Kennedy_International_Airport), provide for the religious needs of airline passengers.[^cite_note-236]

In January 2017, the Ark at JFK Airport, a luxury terminal for pets, opened for \$65 million. Ark was built ostensibly so that people who were transporting pets and other animals would be able to provide luxurious accommodations for these animals. At the time, it was supposed to be the only such facility in the U.S.[^cite_note-237] In January 2018, Ark's owner sued the Port Authority for violating a clause that would have given Ark the exclusive rights to inspect all animals who arrive at JFK from other countries. In the lawsuit, the owner stated that Ark had incurred significant operational losses because many animals were instead being transported to a [United States Department of Agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Agriculture) facility in [Newburgh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newburgh,_New_York).[^cite_note-238]

### Airport hotels

Several hotels are adjacent to JFK Airport, including the [Courtyard by Marriott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Courtyard_by_Marriott) and the [Crowne Plaza](1166288.md). The former [Ramada Plaza JFK Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramada_Plaza_JFK_Hotel) is Building 144,[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240] and it was formerly the only on-site hotel at JFK Airport.[^cite_note-CHM-241] It was previously a part of [Forte Hotels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forte_Hotels) and previously the [Travelodge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travelodge) New York JFK.[^cite_note-242] Due to its role in housing friends and relatives of aircraft crash victims in the 1990s and 2000s, the hotel became known as the "Heartbreak Hotel".[^cite_note-AdamsonPhilly-243][^cite_note-CNNFamiliarTrag-244] In 2009 the PANYNJ stated in its preliminary 2010 budget that it was closing the hotel due to "declining aviation activity and a need for substantial renovation" and that it expected to save \$1 million per month.[^cite_note-245] The hotel closed on December 1, 2009. Almost 200 employees lost their jobs.[^cite_note-Fickenscher-246]

On July 27, 2015, Governor [Andrew Cuomo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Cuomo) announced in a press conference that the [TWA Flight Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TWA_Flight_Center) building would be used by the [TWA Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TWA_Hotel), a 505-room hotel with 40,000 square feet (3,700 m<sup>2</sup>) of conference, event, or meeting space. The new hotel is estimated to have cost \$265 million. The hotel has a 10,000-square-foot (930 m<sup>2</sup>) observation deck with an infinity pool.[^cite_note-247] Groundbreaking for the hotel occurred on December 15, 2016, and it opened on May 15, 2019.[^cite_note-248]

[Resorts World New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resorts_World_New_York_City) is a [casino hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casino_hotel) that is located near the airport, adjacent to the [Aqueduct Racetrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aqueduct_Racetrack). The hotel is planning to expand into an [integrated resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_resort) if granted a full casino license.[^cite_note-249]

### Law enforcement

Law enforcement at John F. Kennedy International Airport is handled primarily by the [Port Authority of New York and New Jersey Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_of_New_York_and_New_Jersey_Police_Department). In addition to normal uniformed patrol of terminals, concourses, and parking lots, the PAPD provides anti-crime plainclothes units, criminal investigative detective squads, counter-terrorism units, high-value cargo escorts and patrols, dignitary protection, marine patrol of surrounding waters, passenger screening point protection and security, Aircraft Rescue Fire Fighting and community outreach. [^cite_note-250] The PAPD operates alongside partner agencies, including the [Transportation Security Administration](172933.md) (TSA), [U.S. Customs and Border Protection](757040.md) (CBP), and the [New York Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Police_Department).

### Information services

In the immediate vicinity of the airport, parking and other information can be obtained by tuning to a [highway advisory radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highway_advisory_radio) station at 1630 [AM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AM_broadcasting).[^cite_note-251] A second station at 1700 AM provides information on traffic concerns for drivers leaving the airport.

Kennedy Airport, along with the other Port Authority airports ([LaGuardia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LaGuardia) and [Newark](248492.md)), uses a uniform style of signage throughout the airport properties. Yellow signs direct passengers to airline gates, ticketing and other flight services; green signs direct passengers to ground transportation services and black signs lead to restrooms, telephones and other passenger amenities. In addition, the Port Authority operates "Welcome Centers" and taxi dispatch booths in each airline terminal, where staff provide customers with information on taxis, limousines, other ground transportation and hotels.

Former New York City traffic reporter [Bernie Wagenblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Wagenblast) provides the voice for the airport's radio stations and the messages heard on board [AirTrain JFK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AirTrain_JFK) and in its stations.[^cite_note-252]

### Notable staff

**Stephen Abraham**, colloquially known as **Kennedy Steve**, was an [air traffic controller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_traffic_controller) at JFK between 1994 and 2017.[^cite_note-253] Abraham was known for his distinct "informal" tone and controlling style while handling traffic at the airport. Several of his interactions with pilots were recorded and featured on social media platforms, including [YouTube](3524766.md) channels. In 2017, Abraham was awarded the [Dale Wright Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dale_Wright_Award) by the [National Air Traffic Controllers Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Air_Traffic_Controllers_Association) (NATCA) for distinguished professionalism and exceptional career service to NATCA and the [National Airspace System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Airspace_System).[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255] In 2019, he was hired as Airside Operations and Ramp Manager at JFK's Terminal 1.[^cite_note-256] In 2026, he started a YouTube channel.[^cite_note-257]

### Cargo

When ranked by the value of shipments passing through it, JFK is the number three freight gateway in the United States (after the [Port of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Los_Angeles) and the [Port of New York and New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_New_York_and_New_Jersey)), and the number one international air freight gateway in the United States.[^cite_note-USDT1-5] Almost 21% of all U.S. international air freight by value and 9.6% by tonnage moved through JFK in 2008.[^cite_note-556]

The JFK air cargo complex is a [Foreign Trade Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_trade_zones_of_the_United_States), which legally lies outside the [customs area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Customs_area) of the United States.[^cite_note-557] JFK is a major hub for air cargo between the United States and Europe. London, Brussels and Frankfurt are JFK's three top trade routes.[^cite_note-bts-cargo-558] The European airports are mostly a link in a global supply chain, however. The top destination markets for cargo flying out of JFK in 2003 were Tokyo, Seoul and London. Similarly, the top origin markets for imports at JFK were Seoul, Hong Kong, Taipei and London.[^cite_note-bts-cargo-558]

20 cargo airlines operate out of JFK,[^cite_note-bts-cargo-558] among them: [Air ACT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_ACT), [Air China Cargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_China_Cargo), [ABX Air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABX_Air), [Asiana Cargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asiana_Cargo), [Atlas Air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlas_Air), [CAL Cargo Air Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CAL_Cargo_Air_Lines), [Cargolux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cargolux), [Cathay Cargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathay_Cargo), [China Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Airlines), [EVA Air Cargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EVA_Air_Cargo), [Emirates SkyCargo](1692704.md), [Nippon Cargo Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nippon_Cargo_Airlines), [FedEx Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx_Express), [DHL Aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DHL_Aviation), [Kalitta Air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalitta_Air), [Korean Air Cargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_Air_Cargo), [Lufthansa Cargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lufthansa_Cargo), [UPS Airlines](9704675.md), [Southern Air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Air), National Airlines, Icelandair Cargo, and, formerly, [World Airways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Airways). Top 5 carriers together transported 33.1% of all revenue freight in 2005: [American Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines) (10.9% of the total), [FedEx Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx_Express) (8.8%), [Lufthansa Cargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lufthansa_Cargo) (5.2%), [Korean Air Cargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_Air_Cargo) (4.9%), and [China Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Airlines) (3.8%).[^cite_note-559]

There are also some on-demand cargo charter services to JFK, operated by carriers such as [Silk Way West Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_Way_West_Airlines).

Most cargo and maintenance facilities at JFK are located north and west of the main terminal area. [DHL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DHL_Aviation), [FedEx Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx_Express), [Japan Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Airlines), [Lufthansa](97733.md), [Nippon Cargo Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nippon_Cargo_Airlines) and [United Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines) have cargo facilities at JFK.[^cite_note-bts-cargo-558][^cite_note-560] In 2000, [Korean Air](315411.md) Cargo opened a new \$102 million cargo terminal at JFK with total floor area of 81,124 square feet (7,536.7 m<sup>2</sup>) and capability of handling 200,000 tons annually. In 2007, [American Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines) opened a new priority parcel service facility at their Terminal 8, featuring 30-minute drop-offs and pick-ups for priority parcel shipments within the US.[^cite_note-561]

"Thus, in 1951 the Authority adopted a regulation prohibiting use of any of its facilities without permission. Accessed December 24, 2024. "France's twin-jet airliner, the Caravelle, has passed a noise test and received permission to operate at New York International Airport, Idlewild, Queens."

[^cite_note-41]: &#x20;Hudson, Edward. ["French Jet Plane Cracks City's Ban; Caravelle Is First to Land Here After Civil Airport Regulation on Noise Others Were Barred"](https://www.nytimes.com/1957/05/03/archives/french-jet-plane-cracks-citys-ban-caravelle-is-first-to-land-here.html), *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, May 3, 1957. Accessed December 24, 2024. "A French jet airliner broke through New York's own 'sound barrier' yesterday.... The twin-engined plane - exempted from the city's ban against jets - became the first commercial jet to land at a New York City airport. The plane, the 500-mile-an-hour Caravelle, touched down at New York International Airport, Idlewild, Queens, at 3\:47 P. M.... Until permission was granted to Sud Aviation on April 12, the bi-state agency had refused entry to jet aircraft at the city's major airports, including the British Comet and the Boeing 707. This policy was adopted formally in 1951 and was aimed at jet planes with asserted intolerable noise characteristics."

[^cite_note-42]: &#x20;["New Soviet Appeal To Land Jet Plane In City Is Rejected; A Last-Minute Request"](https://www.nytimes.com/1957/09/04/archives/new-soviet-appeal-to-land-jet-plane-in-city-is-rejected-a.html), *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, September 4, 1957. Accessed December 24, 2024. "The State Department turned down today a new Soviet request for permission to land a Soviet jet airliner at Idlewild rather than at McGuire Air Force Base in New Jersey. The department said the Port of New York Authority would not permit the Soviet planes to land at its Idlewild Airport without advance noise-level tests.... The Soviet Embassy lodged last-minute objections to plans for the two TU-104 jet passenger planes to land at McGuire Air Force Base."

Accessed March 27, 2023. The agreements were announced at the Commodore Hotel after a closed-door conference of five and a half hours, called by the Port of New York Authority as a result of three airplane crashes in Elizabeth, N.J., which have taken 116 lives in the last two months and which caused the closing of Newark Airport early Monday morning."

Accessed December 24, 2024. "The oldest terminal at the John F. Kennedy International Airport is closing permanently this weekend after 60 years in operation. Terminal 2 opened back in 1962, back when TWA had its own terminal at the airport.... Terminal 2 joins its neighbors (Terminals 1 and 3, the latter was closed in 2014) in making way for the new Terminal 1 that's expected to open in 2026. Officials broke ground in late 2022 on the new state-of-the-art terminal, which is part of an \$18 billion airport redevelopment project. The soon-closing terminal was also the last remnant of what used to be called Idlewild Airport, which housed Northwest, Braniff and Northeast airlines."

Accessed December 24, 2024. "Terminal 6 at Kennedy International Airport — a crisp island of aesthetic tranquility by the master architect I. M. Pei — is being demolished.... The main pavilion, whose white steel roof seems to float ethereally over cascades of diaphanous green glass, is expected to come down by the end of October.... The Port Authority said the Terminal 6 site must be cleared to make room for “improvements that will better serve travelers and help reduce delays,” meaning additional boarding gates and aircraft parking spots for JetBlue's Terminal 5."
